It is an all-India body, serving the Centre and the states alike.\n\nIts remit stops short of local bodies. Elections to panchayats and municipalities are conducted by a separate **State Election Commission**, provided for by the 73rd and 74th Amendments (1992).","anchor":"constitutional-basis-and-scope"},{"topicId":"polity.election-commission","topic":"Election Commission","title":"Composition","text":"$a6","anchor":"composition"},{"topicId":"polity.election-commission","topic":"Election Commission","title":"Safeguards for independence","text":"The Constitution protects the Commission in three ways.\n\n- **Tenure of the CEC.** The CEC can be removed only in the manner and on the grounds applicable to a Supreme Court judge: a presidential order after both Houses pass a resolution by special majority, for proved misbehaviour or incapacity. So the CEC does not serve at the President's pleasure even though the President appoints.\n- **Service conditions.** They cannot be changed to the CEC's disadvantage after appointment.\n- **Other members.** An election commissioner or regional commissioner can be removed only on the CEC's recommendation. The judge-like protection therefore belongs to the CEC alone.\n\nRecognised weaknesses in the constitutional scheme are that it sets no qualifications for members, does not specify their term, and does not bar retired commissioners from further government posts.","anchor":"safeguards-for-independence"},{"topicId":"polity.election-commission","topic":"Election Commission","title":"Anoop Baranwal (2023)","text":"In Anoop Baranwal (2023) the Supreme Court issued directions to shield the Commission from executive and political influence.\n\n| Subject | Direction |\n|---|---|\n| Appointment | CEC and other commissioners to be appointed on the recommendation of a three-member committee: the Prime Minister, the Leader of the Opposition in the Lok Sabha (or, if none, the leader of the largest opposition party), and the Chief Justice of India |\n| Removal of other commissioners | Desirably on the same grounds as the CEC, that is like a Supreme Court judge, on the CEC's recommendation |\n| Service conditions | Not to be altered to the disadvantage of other commissioners after appointment |\n\nThe directions are to apply until Parliament enacts a law in line with Article 324. Readers should check the current legal position, since Parliament has legislated on the subject.","anchor":"anoop-baranwal-2023"},{"topicId":"polity.election-commission","topic":"Election Commission","title":"Powers and functions","text":"$a7","anchor":"powers-and-functions"},{"topicId":"polity.election-commission","topic":"Election Commission","title":"Reading the powers together","text":"A useful way to remember the Commission's role is to follow an election in time order. Before the poll it fixes constituencies on the basis of delimitation law, keeps the voter list current, recognises parties and allots symbols. During the campaign it enforces the code of conduct and allocates broadcast time to parties. On polling and counting it supervises the whole machinery, may cancel a poll that has been spoiled by rigging, booth capturing or violence, and borrows staff from governments when needed. Afterwards it still has an advisory duty: the President and Governors seek its opinion on disqualification of legislators.\n\nNote the limits. The Commission does not decide election petitions about the result; those go to courts. It handles only disputes about party recognition and symbols as a quasi-judicial authority. It also cannot supervise local-body polls, which belong to the State Election Commissions.","anchor":"reading-the-powers-together"},{"topicId":"polity.election-commission","topic":"Election Commission","title":"Machinery under the Commission","text":"At the top, deputy election commissioners drawn from the civil service, on tenure, assist the Commission, supported by secretaries, joint secretaries, deputy secretaries and under secretaries in its secretariat. In each state a chief electoral officer is appointed by the CEC in consultation with the state government. At district level the collector serves as district returning officer, who names a returning officer for each constituency and a presiding officer for each polling booth.","anchor":"machinery-under-the-commission"},{"topicId":"polity.election-commission","topic":"Election Commission","title":"Vision and mission","text":"The Commission's Strategic Plan (2016–2025) describes the Commission as striving to be an institution of excellence that deepens electoral democracy. It commits to independence, integrity and autonomy and to free, fair and transparent elections.
